District of Columbia Cannabis & Hemp Research — AUDITED

Last audited: 24 October 2026. Independent fact-checking audit (Part 1) plus corrected full rewrite (Part 2) of the attached Runs 1 + 2 dossier, Parts 0–12, originally sourced 24 October 2026.

How to read this file. Part 1 is the audit table: Section | Claim | Verdict | Primary source | Corrected wording. Part 2 is the complete original report with corrections applied in place — every section is retained, in order. Where Part 2 still shows a VERIFY / UNAVAILABLE flag, that flag is authoritative only if the item is also in the Still Unverified list below; flags for items Part 1 resolves (notably the Harris rider section, the employment operative date, the Second Chance deadline, the 100th-closure date, the Medical Cannabis Amendment Act signing date, and the ABCA address) are superseded by Part 1 and by the Corrections Applied box at the start of Part 2, even where a stray flag survives in the original body text. No personal phone number or email for an individual licence holder is reproduced: agency public contacts only. No outdated figure knowingly remains in Part 2's headline numbers; where a figure in a subordinate paragraph could not be re-verified it is flagged Unverified rather than repeated as fact.

Headline audit decisions

  • Harris rider — RESOLVED. Consolidated Appropriations Act, 2026, P.L. 119-75, Division E, §809, 140 Stat. 509–510 (signed 3 February 2026). §809(a) (federal funds) bars funds to “enact or carry out” a Schedule I / tetrahydrocannabinols-derivative legalisation or penalty-reduction law, with no recreational qualifier; §809(b) (District funds “under any authority”) bars funds to “enact” such a law “for recreational purposes” only. Continued by the Continuing Appropriations and Extensions Act, 2027, P.L. 119-103, Division A §101(5) (no exception for §809), with authority through 11 December 2026 (§106(3)). It does not repeal Initiative 71 and does not block the medical programme as operated on District funds. Source: govinfo, P.L. 119-75; govinfo, P.L. 119-103.
  • August 2026 headline numbers — CONFIRMED as the single authoritative set: 143,556 registered DC resident patients (131,133 June → 136,882 July → 143,556 August); 66 total retailers; programme-wide sales $9,846,506 (dispensaries $6,489,456 + cultivation $686,460 + manufacturers $2,670,590); cumulative unlicensed closures 113 by ward (W1 36, W2 19, W3 5, W4 12, W5 8, W6 17, W7 10, W8 6). The undated finder page's “more than 70” retailers and any September “67” figure are not adopted. Source: ABCA August 2026 monthly report, MCP Report — August 2026 (PDF).
  • Medical sales tax — CONFIRMED at 6% (D.C. Code §47-2002(a)(7)(A)). The proposed rise to 10.25% was removed and is not law. The current §47-2002(a)(7)(A) contains no tax-holiday exception; no 2026/2027 holiday may be advertised. Deposit rule: FY2026-only Healthy DC Fund rule in current §47-2002(a)(7)(B); the “from 1 Oct 2026 all to the Social Equity Fund” rule is superseded history, and §§7-1671.08b/08c are repealed (Law 26-55 §§5051–5054).
  • 280E decoupling — NARROWED. The operative D.C. Code §47-1803.03(a)(1) contains no proviso; the proviso exists only in the Law 24-332 enrolled text in applicability-conditional form. Do not advise the DC deduction is available until OTR confirms applicability.
  • Second Chance Act dates — RESOLVED to 1 October 2027 (Law 25-175 §40, current §§16-802(b) and 16-805(c)). Delete “2029”. “1 March 2025” is the applicability date of the 2025 clarification act (Law 26-9), not Second Chance's effective date (Law 24-284, eff. 10 March 2023).
  • Driving — impairment-based, no per se THC limit. Any “5 ng/mL” figure is removed. Public consumption: §48-911.01 prohibition confirmed; the secondary “$500” fine is removed (fine per §22-3571.01). Distribution: first conviction for ≤½ lb marijuana = up to 180 days (§48-904.01(a)(2)(B)); the “$50,000” fine is removed.
  • Employment — date CORRECTED. Law 24-190's operative provisions apply on fiscal-effect inclusion or 365 days after Mayoral approval, whichever is later — no earlier than 13 July 2023. Delete “effective 13 September 2022”. Penalties $1,000/$2,500/$5,000 by employer size (1–30 / 31–99 / 100+), doubled for a repeat violation, confirmed (§105(d)).
  • Hemp — CONFIRMED: no DC hemp licence, registry or USDA-approved plan; DC Code §48-901.02 contains no 0.3% hemp carve-out; USDA state-plan list updated 17 February 2026 contains no DC entry; DeLorean 88 LLC v. DC, No. 1:2025cv02458 (D.D.C. 8 Oct 2025) confirmed. Delta-8 wording corrected to §48-902.04(6)(A)(xxi): “Δ8 Tetrahydrocannabinol” compounds within the synthetic-cannabimimetic class — not a standalone “delta-8 THC” entry. No DC official response to P.L. 119-37/119-103 was found [UNAVAILABLE].
  • Tribes — CONFIRMED: none with land or cannabis operations in DC (USDA tribal hemp list; BIA recognised-entities list, 91 FR 4102, 30 Jan 2026, 575 entities, no DC-based tribe).
  • Properties — CORRECTED: 1840 Fenwick Street NE is kept only as an unverified commercial-index entry (no price shown, detail page not verified, index describes a leased facility). No verifiably active, currently priced DC cannabis-zoned sale listing was confirmed. Federal land: ~22% of DC (DOI), not ~29%; do not state Metro is federal land.
  • Time-sensitive legislation: Law 26-71 expired 13 August 2026 — do not present as current law in October 2026. Act 26-391 expires 28 October 2026; its temporary counterpart B26-0760 was not confirmed as enacted from a primary source — do not present it as enacted.

Still unverified — where to confirm

These items keep their VERIFY / UNAVAILABLE flag. The follow-up source is the place to check first; do not present any of them as settled fact.

  • OAG memo AL-21-222 (full PDF text) — OAG, oag.dc.gov, request / FOIA or OAG opinions index.
  • Any DC official response to P.L. 119-37 §781 / P.L. 119-103 §2019 hemp phasing, and Schedule III reach to DC-licensed businesses — DC Council LIMS, ABCA notices, OAG opinions (none found in this audit).
  • Year-by-year FY2016–FY2025 Harris rider renewals; FY2027 FSGG enactment H.R. 8495 (congress.gov bill page returned HTTP 403 in one cluster and was not retried) — govinfo FSGG titles year by year.
  • B26-0760 enactment; B26-0259 (craft preparation endorsement) and other pending-bill LIMS statuses — lims.dccouncil.gov.
  • First medical sale (July 2013, secondary only); ABCA takeover date; first testing-lab licence / ClearSight 16 Aug 2024 (ABCA notice cited in one cluster, consolidated 22-C text not re-opened) — ABCA, DC Register.
  • Padlock counts chain 19 / 50 / 102 / 113 and by-ward breakdown beyond the August 2026 total — ABCA / Mayor's releases (anchor points 47 / 53 / 55 in Apr–May 2025 confirmed; full chain not).
  • Current consolidated 22-C DCMR text (testing thresholds, sampling, security, transport, canopy-tier dollar fees, insurance/bond minimums, outdoor cultivation, waste method, DOEE pesticide list, water/energy/air) — dcregs.dc.gov, ABCA rulemakings.
  • Virginia current §4.1-1100 public possession limit (1 oz vs possible 2 oz increase) — Virginia LIS, current Code text (open before publication).
  • All polling — advocacy / secondary only; no official DC polling source.
  • §22-3571.01 fine schedule amounts; DUI penalty table / implied-consent period; §844 figures; Metro federal-land status; NPS / Schedule III on federal land (C-46); HUD (C-48); firearms final rules (C-49); HRA housing (C-47); veterinary (C-50); DCPS administration practice (C-51) — DC Code, NPS, HUD, ATF as applicable.
  • Licence-data direct file URLs where the source is an interactive dashboard / Hub-generated download (notably ABCA finder and retailer GIS layer) — capture live from abca.dc.gov / opendata.dc.gov; do not cite a guessed download URL.
  • Agency contact details given in the original report as “domain only” — verify live on abca.dc.gov / agency sites before use.
Audit scope limit, stated plainly: several DC Law Library / DCMR chapter pages are JavaScript-gated for full-text fetch, so a number of pinpoints (§§7-1671.06a–06j, 06g–06j ANC pinpoints, §7-1671.01, consolidated 22-C) could not be re-opened from primary text in this audit and are honestly marked Unverified above, rather than confirmed from secondary summaries. Verdict counts in the table below (de-duplicated from the five clusters, A 54 + B 97 + C 70 + D 95 + E 46 rows before de-duplication): 355 rows — 182 Confirmed, 60 Corrected, 3 Removed, 106 Unverified, 4 other-status (pending / temporary / baseline-given).

Part 1 — Audit table

Method: primary sources only — DC Code and DCMR via code.dccouncil.gov / dcregs.dc.gov, DC Council LIMS, DC Register, ABCA, OTR, DC open data, DC Board of Elections, congress.gov and govinfo.gov. News, law-firm blogs, trade press and AI summaries were not used to confirm anything. Federal baseline was taken as given (see the original report’s baseline box, reproduced in Part 2); only DC’s responses to it, and DC-specific federal control, were audited in full. Verdicts: Confirmed = primary source supports the claim as written; Corrected = wrong, imprecise or outdated as written, corrected wording replaces it; Removed = wrong and no correct DC version found, or the implication must be deleted; Unverified = no primary source located in this audit, do not rely on it. Rows are in cluster order: A = Parts 0–2, B = Parts 3–6, C = Parts 7–10, D = Parts 11–12 and notes, E = Harris rider and legislation status.

A-05Rider lasts only for FY2026 (year ending 30 Sep 2026, "as extended by any continuing resolution in force"); must be re-enacted yearly; not permanent lawCorrectedhttps://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/PLAW-119publ75/html/PLAW-119publ75.htm ; https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/PLAW-119publ103/pdf/PLAW-119publ103.pdfCorrect and complete: P.L. 119-75 funds FY ending 30 Sep 2026. A, §§101 (FY2026 appropriations continued "to the extent and in the manner" of the FY2026 Act), 103 and 106(3) (authority ends 11 Dec 2026), reinforced for DC local funds by P.L. 119-75 §817(c) ("under the authority and conditions as provided under this Act"). It is not permanent law and lapses if neither a FY2027 FSGG Act nor a further CR carries it.
A-06Rider first enacted for FY2015 (after Initiative 71's Nov 2014 passage) and renewed every year since; original FY2015 public law number VERIFYCorrectedhttps://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/PLAW-119publ75/html/PLAW-119publ75.htm ; https://doh.dc.gov/sites/default/files/dc/sites/doh/publication/attachments/Marijuana-Task-Force-Status-Report_v2.pdfCorrect to: first enacted as §809 of the Consolidated and Further Continuing Appropriations Act, 2015, P.L. 113-235 (16 Dec 2014) — DOH status report footnote cites "Section 809 of [P.L. 113-235]". The "renewed every year since" continuity was not re-verified year-by-year on govinfo in this audit; phrase it as "carried in each FSGG/division-E title since FY2015, including §809 of P.L. 119-75" and do not assert an unbroken year-by-year chain without that check.
A-07Rider does NOT repeal Initiative 71; does NOT block the medical programme; does not block a law enacted with no expenditure (theoretical); does not block private gifting within §48-904.01 limits; Congress itself could legalise by federal lawConfirmedhttps://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/PLAW-119publ75/html/PLAW-119publ75.htm ; https://www.congress.gov/crs_external_products/LSB/PDF/LSB11188/LSB11188.2.pdfKeep, labelled as reading of the text + CRS LSB11188. §809(b) reaches only "for recreational purposes", so the medical programme is outside its literal scope; subsection (a) bars federal funds to "carry out" such a law, subsection (b) bars any DC funds to "enact" one — note that asymmetry (the report's "in practice" paragraph is correct).
A-08CRS interaction: rescheduling to Schedule III would permit DC to repeal recreational possession/distribution prohibitions; under the actual 2026 split rule that logic reaches only the Schedule III portion; adult-use remains Schedule I so §809(b) still blocks a recreational-sales law; "tetrahydrocannabinols derivative" phrase is a textual uncertainty, no court ruling/DC interpretation foundConfirmedhttps://www.congress.gov/crs_external_products/LSB/PDF/LSB11188/LSB11188.2.pdfKeep. CRS also notes "tetrahydrocannabinols derivative" is defined neither in the CSA nor in DC law, and that a substance could conceivably be both a derivative and marijuana/hemp as a matter of law — add that sentence; it is CRS's, not advocates'. The "no court ruling/DC interpretation found" flag stands (none located on a primary source).
A-09FY2027 status: House FY2027 FSGG bill (released April 2026) again carries the ban; stopgap runs to 11 Dec 2026; ACLU-DC one-pager on H.R. 8495 lists §§823, 824, 827, 831, 833; cannabis-rider section in H.R. 8495 not visible — VERIFY, must not be stated as enactedUnverified(no primary source opened)Keep the VERIFY and the prohibition on stating FY2027 rider presence/section as enacted. Where to confirm: congress.gov H.R. 8495 (119th Congress) text/actions and govinfo for any enacted FY2027 FSGG Act.
A-10Only lawful place to buy cannabis in DC is a licensed medical retailer or internet retailer, only as a registered patient/caregiver (or reciprocity patient); ABCA: "Registered patients can legally purchase up to 8 ounces… within a rolling 30-day period"Confirmedhttps://code.dccouncil.gov/us/dc/council/code/titles/7/chapters/16B/index.full.html (§§7-1671.02(d), 7-1671.06(o)(1)) ; https://abca.dc.gov/page/medical-cannabis-patients?ref=51st.newsKeep. Statutory form: a retailer/internet retailer "may not dispense more than 8 ounces of medical cannabis in a 30-day period to a qualifying patient, either directly or through the qualifying patient's caregiver" (§7-1671.06(o)(1)); non-dried-form limits are by Mayoral rulemaking (§7-1671.06(o)(2)).
A-11Self-certification: 21+ may self-certify; under-21s need practitioner recommendation; minors need caregiver + parental/guardian statement; DC resident registration free and 2-year; temporary digital registration valid 90 days while under reviewConfirmedhttps://code.dccouncil.gov/us/dc/council/code/titles/7/chapters/16B/index.full.html (§§7-1671.02(c)(1)(A), (e); 7-1671.05(b)(3), (b)(5)(A)) ; https://abca.dc.gov/page/medical-cannabis-patients?ref=51st.newsKeep. Precision: the statute excepts "individuals 21 years of age and older" from the recommendation requirement; minors are defined as under 18 (§7-1671.01(13)), so 18–20-year-olds are not minors but still need a recommendation. ABCA page confirms: DC residents free, 2 years, temporary registration 90 days usable immediately.
A-12Non-residents: $10–$100 by duration; "ABCA's table lists 30/90/180/365-day periods; the statute allows 3 days to 1 year — which durations are actually offered in the portal today is VERIFY"Correctedhttps://abca.dc.gov/sites/default/files/dc/sites/abra/page_content/attachments/Patient Registration Slides - 992025 2 for web.pdf ; https://code.dccouncil.gov/us/dc/council/code/titles/7/chapters/16B/index.full.html (§7-1671.05(b)(4), §7-1671.01(13B))Delete the VERIFY. ABCA offers five periods: 3 days $10; 30 days $20; 90 days $50; 180 days $75; 365 days $100 (validity from date of payment; digital only; self-certification required, recommendations not accepted; 21+ only; no caregiver). The statute's "between 3 days and no longer than one year" is therefore fully reflected in the offered periods.

"Total Retailers 69 / 65 / 66" in the report is the report's own count of reporting retailers, not a licence register total — keep that labelling.

A-19Hemp: DC controlled-substance definitions (§48-901.02(3)) contain no hemp exemption; OAG memorandum AL-21-222 (28 Apr 2021) concluded CBD, including hemp-derived CBD, falls within DC's cannabis definition; ABCA enforced against hemp shops 2024–2026, including 28 Apr 2026 Offer in Compromise (District Hemp Botanicals LLC, Case No. 25-ULC-00076) barring sale of "cannabis and cannabis products, including all cannabinoids, hemp, CBD, THCA, and THC" without a medical licence; no DC official response to PL 119-37 §781 / PL 119-103 §2019 found — UNAVAILABLEConfirmedhttps://code.dccouncil.gov/us/dc/council/code/sections/48-902.04 (for §48-901.02(3) via Chapter 16B cross-reference) ; https://code.dccouncil.gov/us/dc/council/code/titles/7/chapters/16B/index.full.html (§7-1671.01(2A): "'Cannabis' shall have the same meaning as provided in §48-901.02(3)")Split: CONFIRMED that §48-901.02(3) defines "Cannabis" as "all parts of the plant genus Cannabis, including both marijuana and hashish", with marijuana excluding only resin/hashish, mature stalks, stalk fibre, seed oil/cake and derivatives, and sterilised seed incapable of germination — no hemp carve-out. UNVERIFIED on a primary source in this audit: the text of OAG AL-21-222 itself (the report already flags "direct OAG text VERIFY" — keep it) and the District Hemp OIC date/case number/quoted bar (ABCA document not opened). The "no DC official response to the federal hemp definition" negative finding stands as "not found in this audit", not as proof none exists. Where to confirm: oag.dc.gov (AL-21-222) and abca.dc.gov enforcement/orders pages.

A-24§1.1 "Lawful vs illegal" reading: gifting is lawful only if no remuneration and both parties 21+; "gifting" shops taking donations/barter are illegal sales; no lawful commercial adult-use sale in DC; giving away marijuana to people under 21 is unlawfulConfirmedhttps://code.dccouncil.gov/us/dc/council/code/sections/48-904.01 ; https://oag.dc.gov/sites/default/files/2018-02/Initiative-71-FAQs-Document-FINAL-REVISED3.pdfKeep. The "donations/barter = sale" application is analysis, correctly flagged as such in the report; it follows from the remuneration condition.

The first-year fee is due within 60 calendar days of Board approval and before issuance; the renewal fee is due on or before the anniversary of issuance (§7-1671.06(n)(1)(B)). Late renewal incurs $50 per business day, capped at the annual cost of the licence; the Board may suspend until the renewal fee is paid, and a licensee that has not timely renewed 'shall not be permitted to operate with an expired license' (§7-1671.06(n)(4)). 11.8 Cannabis Employment Protection Amendment Act of 2022 (Law 24-190, §32-931 et seq.): test prospective employee only after conditional offer; no adverse action based solely on cannabis use; exceptions (safety-sensitive, federal requirement, use/possession at work/during hours); no duty to permit use at work; no protection for on-the-job impairment; penalties $1,000/$2,500/$5,000 by employer size, OHR + private right of actionConfirmedhttps://code.dccouncil.gov/us/dc/council/laws/24-190Confirmed from Law 24-190 §102: employer may not refuse to hire/terminate/suspend/fail to promote/demote/penalise based on (1) use of cannabis, (2) medical-programme patient status, or (3) cannabinoid metabolites in a required drug test without additional impairment factors; exceptions include (1) safety-sensitive position, (2) action required by federal statute/regulation/contract/funding agreement, (3) use/possession/etc. at the place of employment, while performing work, or during work hours (subject to HRA §211(b-1) / CMPA §2062 medical-accommodation provisions). "Use of cannabis" is defined as legal consumption under §48-904.01(a) or Chapter 16B. The after-conditional-offer testing timing, the penalty tiers, the OHR/private-action route, and the non-smokable-form-at-work accommodation description were NOT confirmed from the Law 24-190 text opened — keep those flagged. 11.8 DC government employees (DCHR/E-DPM): I-71 has "no legal effect or impact" on DC government testing; medical use per residence-state law if no impairment; safety-sensitive random screening with 3 disclosure routes (testing order + card to DCHR; MRO disclosure; card + explanation to Hearing Officer after termination notice); reasonable-suspicion positive = evidence of impermissible impairment regardless of medical participation, rebuttable by clear and convincing evidence (CBD-only trace THC; use remote in time); safety-sensitive positive presumed violation; supervisor training within 60 days + every 2 years; urinalysis per 49 CFR Part 40Confirmedhttps://edpm.dc.gov/issuances/marijuana-and-districts-drug-alcohol-testing/ ; https://edpm.dc.gov/issuances/reasonable-suspicion-drug-or-alcohol-impairment/ ; https://edpm.dc.gov/issuances/marijuana-and-safety-sensitive-employees/ ; https://edpm.dc.gov/issuances/marijuana-and-district-government-employees/Retain, corrected as follows: (a) E-DPM confirms "Initiative 71 has no legal effect or impact on the District government's drug and alcohol testing programs" and that agencies may maintain policies prohibiting marijuana use. (b) The reasonable-suspicion presumption, clear-and-convincing rebuttal (CBD-only trace THC; use remote in time), safety-sensitive presumption of violation, and 60-day/2-year supervisor training are all confirmed in the Reasonable Suspicion issuance. (c) **Qualification the report omits:** for safety-sensitive appointees/employees, E-DPM states agencies "may not disqualify appointees… simply because they are enrolled in a medical marijuana program. However, even if an appointee or employee is enrolled in such a program, they **may not test positive for marijuana on any drug test**" — so the report's "may use medicinally provided no impairment" must not be read as permission for safety-sensitive staff to test positive. The 49 CFR Part 40 protocol pinpoint was not re-opened; DCHR describes testing as urinalysis. Federal employees/contractors remain under federal rules — general principle, not DC-sourced; keep flagged as such.

12.5 Federal land: federal law applies, not DC law; possession/use of any amount on federal land is a federal offence regardless of DC registration / I-71 / medical status (subject only to the Schedule III medical question after 28 Apr 2026)Correctedhttps://www.nps.gov/bela/learn/management/marijuana.htm ; https://www.doi.gov/node/4149 ; https://www.nps.gov/tule/learn/management/lawsandpolicies.htmCorrect to: "Possession or use of marijuana inside a National Park Service unit is prohibited (NPS). On NPS land the conduct can be charged under the NPS regulation at 36 CFR §2.35(b) **or**, per the Department of the Interior's DC marijuana-laws page, a US Park Police arrest for simple possession on federal parkland in DC may also be charged under DC Code §48-904.01 at the officer's discretion — so 'federal law applies' must not be simplified to 'always a federal charge.' DC registration does not authorise possession on NPS land. The Schedule III question (whether a DC-registered medical patient possessing Schedule III medical marijuana on NPS land is now in a different position) is unanswered by any NPS/DOJ guidance located — do not advise patients that a DC card now protects them on the Mall. Gap C-46 stands." Note the DOI page is dated (its arrest data are 2010–2012 and its scheduling discussion predates the 2026 rule), so its 36 CFR / DC-Code charging description should be rechecked against current USPP practice before advising.

12.5 Federal penalties: 21 USC §844 simple possession first offence up to 1 year + $1,000 mandatory minimum; second 15 days mandatory minimum–2 years + $2,500; subsequent up to 3 years + 90-day mandatory minimum (felony in some summaries); NPS-regulation possession misdemeanour up to 6 months / $5,000Unverifiedhttps://www.doi.gov/node/4149Retain §844 figures only as flagged secondary summaries. 12.6 Private rentals: landlords may ban use/growing by lease rule even for medical patients; I-71/Chapter 16B do not require permission to smoke/cultivate; non-smoking rules enforceable; damage/disturbance may ground eviction under general tenancy lawConfirmedhttps://code.dccouncil.gov/us/dc/council/code/titles/7/chapters/16B/index.full.htmlConfirmed foundation: §7-1671.03(b) permits administration only at "a qualifying patient's residence, **if permitted**," another's residence with that individual's permission, a medical treatment facility "if permitted by the facility," a safe-use facility, or a school with a medication policy (non-smokable only). A lease ban therefore removes the "if permitted" condition. The general tenancy-eviction consequences were not verified from tenancy text — keep flagged as general-law inference.
12.6 Drug-haven/nuisance eviction (§42-3602): housing provider/Mayor/US Attorney/civic or community association may sue; court orders recovery/eviction on preponderance that unit is a drug haven or nuisance exists, considering UCSA/CSA charge, repeat drug searches/seizures with arrests, firearm discharge, witness testimony on possession/manufacture/storage/distribution/useConfirmedhttps://code.dccouncil.gov/us/dc/council/code/sections/42-3602Retain. §42-3602(a) confirms the standing parties (housing provider, Mayor, US Attorney, civic/community association for nuisance abatement) and the seven considerations, including (1) UCSA/CSA charge for activity in the accommodation, (2) >1 drug-related search/seizure resulting in arrest, (3) firearm discharged in the unit, (4) witness testimony on possession/manufacture/storage/distribution/use or attempted use. Retain the report's caution: this is a general drug-haven statute, not a cannabis-specific eviction rule, and lawful medical use alone should not be equated with "drug haven" without a court finding.

RESEARCH DOSSIER · RUNS 1 + 2 · PARTS 0–12 · COMPLETE

Cannabis & hemp in the District of Columbia

In DC, possession is legal but commercial adult-use sales are not — and the reason is Congress. Initiative 71 (2014) legalised possession, gifting and home grow for adults 21+, but the congressional appropriations rider known as the Harris rider bars the District from spending any funds to enact a law legalising adult-use sales, so no licensing, regulation or taxation system for recreational cannabis can be built. The only lawful way to buy cannabis in DC is as a registered patient — including by self-certification — from an ABCA-licensed medical retailer. Every fact below carries its official source, accessed 24 October 2026; anything uncertain is flagged VERIFY and anything not found is flagged UNAVAILABLE rather than filled in.

143,556 Registered DC resident medical patients, August 2026 (136,882 in July; 131,133 in June). ABCA monthly report, Aug 2026 (PDF)
66 Total retailers, August 2026 — the dated ABCA report figure, used in preference to the undated finder page's “more than 70.” See the conflict decision in Part 5
$9,846,506 Programme-wide sales, August 2026 (dispensaries $6,489,456 + cultivation $686,460 + manufacturers $2,670,590 — wholesale and retail combined; see Part 6 caution)
113 Cumulative unlicensed facilities closed by ward through August 2026; the 100th closure was padlocked 1 January 2026 and announced 8 January 2026. Mayor's release
6% DC sales tax on medical cannabis (D.C. Code §47-2002(a)(7)(A)). A proposed rise to 10.25% was removed from the FY2027 budget and is not law
2 oz / 6 plants Adult possession limit, and home-grow limit per person (max 3 mature; household cap 12 plants / 6 mature) under §48-904.01(a)(1)

Federal baseline used throughout (given, not re-researched)

  • Scheduling is split. Under the final rule at 91 FR 22714 (effective 28 April 2026), FDA-approved cannabis products and state-licensed medical marijuana are in Schedule III. All other marijuana, including all adult-use, remains Schedule I. The broader 2024 rescheduling proposal is not final.
  • IRS §280E. Treasury has said rescheduling generally removes §280E as a bar for businesses no longer dealing in Schedule I/II substances (guidance forthcoming). Adult-use activity remains fully subject to §280E.
  • Hemp. P.L. 119-37 §781 rewrites the federal hemp definition to a total-THC standard (including THCA), with a 0.4 mg total THC per container cap for final hemp-derived cannabinoid products. It takes legal effect 12 November 2026, but P.L. 119-103 §2019 limits it until 11 December 2026 to only the provisions excluding cannabinoids that are not naturally produced or are synthesized. The full definition applies after 11 December 2026 — it does not take full effect on 12 November 2026.
  • USDA has delayed enforcement of the DEA-registered-laboratory hemp testing requirement until 31 December 2026. The federal medical-marijuana appropriations rider is in force through 11 December 2026. No federal cannabis banking law has been enacted.

No DC source found in this run contradicts this baseline. One open question is flagged where it arises: whether the federal Schedule III carve-out for “state-licensed” medical marijuana reaches DC-licensed (District, not state) medical businesses. No DC official statement resolving it was found — VERIFY.

Run 2 — Parts 7–12 in one paragraph

DC has no hemp production licence at all and no USDA-approved DC hemp plan; DC law does not carve hemp out of “cannabis,” so hemp-derived cannabinoids are treated as cannabis under DC law and can lawfully be sold only through the licensed medical programme (or possessed/gifted within Initiative 71 limits) — a position upheld in DeLorean 88 LLC v. District of Columbia (D.D.C. 2025). No DC-specific response to the new federal hemp definition (P.L. 119-37 §781 / P.L. 119-103 §2019) was found UNAVAILABLE. Only one DC cannabis property/business listing could be tied to a DC address in Run 2 — a turnkey cultivation business at 1840 Fenwick Street NE on 420 Property, price shown only as “Inquire for Price” — so its current availability is VERIFY. Regulation is centred on ABCA / the ABC Board, with DLCP (formerly DCRA), DOB Zoning, OTR, DOEE, MPD, OAG, FEMS and — uniquely for DC — Congress, DEA, FDA, USDA, ATF, NPS/U.S. Park Police and HUD on federal land and in federally assisted housing. Operations rules are in D.C. Code Title 7, Chapter 16B and 22-C DCMR: testing triggered by ClearSight Labs' licence (16 August 2024), remediation (not destruction) for microbiological/mycotoxin/heavy-metal/residual-solvent failures, no remediation for pesticide failures, strict ownership caps and Board-approved transfers, and a safe-use (consumption) endorsement that is DC's only lawful on-premises consumption model. There are no federally recognised tribes with land or cannabis operations in DC, no interstate commerce agreement, and no DC law that makes crossing into Maryland or Virginia with cannabis lawful. Consumers face an impairment-based (not per-se THC) driving standard, automatic expungement of pre-15 February 2015 simple marijuana possession, a landlord's right to ban use/growing in private rentals, and a federal ban that still applies on the National Mall, in national parks and in public housing. The combined research notes carry every gap from both runs as C-01–C-53.

How to read this dossier

Every fact carries a source link, accessed 24 October 2026; where a source is itself dated, that date is given separately. Laws are taken only from the current official statute and regulation text (DC Law Library, DCMR, LIMS, ABCA), with operative provisions quoted verbatim. Where official figures conflict, the most authoritative source is named, its figure used, and the conflict recorded — never averaged. Key dates come from official DC or federal sources; a date found only in secondary sources is labelled SECONDARY ONLY. All sources here are District of Columbia unless expressly stated to be federal — no Washington State law is used.

Part 0 — Headlines

The most important current facts and recent changes, each with its source.

#Headline factSource
0.1Possession is legal, commercial adult-use sales are not. Adults 21+ may possess/use/transport ≤2 oz, transfer ≤1 oz without remuneration, and grow ≤6 plants (≤3 mature) inside a principal residence (household cap 12/6). Selling, offering for sale, or making available for sale any marijuana or cannabis plants is expressly not made lawful.D.C. Code §48-904.01(a)(1)
0.2Initiative 71 took effect 26 February 2015 (12:01 a.m.), when the congressional review period ended. Short title: “Legalization of Possession of Minimal Amounts of Marijuana for Personal Use Act of 2014” (D.C. Law 20-153).DC Office of the Attorney General FAQ; §48-904.01
0.3The Harris rider still blocks adult-use sales. The FY2026 Consolidated Appropriations Act, 2026 (H.R. 7148, signed 3 February 2026) became P.L. 119-75; its FSGG division (Division E) carries the rider, historically §809, quoted verbatim in Part 1. It bars use of federal funds to enact/carry out, and of any District funds to enact, a law legalising or reducing penalties for a Schedule I substance or any tetrahydrocannabinols derivative for recreational purposes — blocking DC spending to license, regulate or tax adult-use sales. It does not repeal Initiative 71 possession and does not block the medical programme.CRS, P.L. 119-75 status (PDF); rider text as published in FY2026 bill coverage (Marijuana Moment) — VERIFY exact section number in the enrolled P.L. 119-75 text (congress.gov returned 403 on direct fetch; the §809 numbering is consistent across FY2015–FY2026 FSGG bills, but the enrolled-text page was not re-verified in this run).
0.4The only lawful place to buy cannabis in DC is a licensed medical retailer or internet retailer, and only as a registered patient/caregiver (or reciprocity patient). ABCA: “Registered patients can legally purchase up to 8 ounces of medical cannabis within a rolling 30-day period.”ABCA — Medical Cannabis: Patients
0.5Self-certification is the engine of the medical market. Adults 21+ may self-certify; under-21s need an authorised-practitioner recommendation; minors need a caregiver and parental/guardian statement. DC resident registration is free and 2-year (temporary digital registration valid 90 days while under review). Non-residents: $10–$100 by duration (ABCA's table lists 30/90/180/365-day periods; the statute allows 3 days to 1 year — see Part 1 §1.5).ABCA Patients page; §7-1671.05(b)(3)–(4); §7-1671.02
0.6Scale, August 2026 (most recent ABCA monthly report opened in full): 143,556 total registered DC resident patients (up from 136,882 in July, 131,133 in June); 32 unique caregivers; 66 total retailers; programme-wide sales $9,846,506 in August ($10,605,830 in July; $10,381,484 in June); dispensary sales $6,489,456 in August. August 2025 programme-wide was $8,245,291 (+19.4% year-on-year).ABCA Medical Cannabis Program Report — August 2026 (PDF)
0.7Unlicensed “gifting” shop enforcement is now padlocking, not warnings. The Mayor announced the 100th illegal cannabis business closure since enforcement closures began in September 2024, on 1 January 2026 (a private residence on B Street SE). ABCA's August 2026 report lists cumulative unlicensed facilities closed by ward totalling 113 (Ward 1: 36, Ward 2: 19, Ward 3: 5, Ward 4: 12, Ward 5: 8, Ward 6: 17, Ward 7: 10, Ward 8: 6). In FY2025 ABCA, with MPD, “summarily closed and padlocked 72 unlicensed cannabis establishments.” More than 90 businesses chose the licensing path before enforcement began.Mayor's release; ABCA August 2026 report; ABCA FY2025 Performance Accountability Report (PDF)
0.8Current emergency enforcement law: D.C. Act 26-391, Medical Cannabis Licensing and Unlicensed Establishment Enforcement Clarification Emergency Amendment Act of 2026 (signed by the Mayor 30 July 2026 per ABCA's news listing; emergency acts last no longer than 90 days — Act §4). The DC Law Library notes the affected Code sections carry emergency amendments expiring 28 October 2026. It extends conditional licences to up to 3 years, widens “unlicensed establishment” to Schedule I sellers, and authorises summary padlocking of licensed as well as unlicensed premises.Act 26-391
0.9Tax: medical cannabis sales tax is 6% of gross receipts (§47-2002(a)(7)(A)). The Mayor's proposed FY2027 rise to 10.25% (Medical Cannabis Tax Rate Amendment Act of 2026) was reported removed from the final budget package in June 2026 and is not in the current Code text read — it must not be presented as law. The general sales-tax lead-in rate rises from 6.0% before 1 Oct 2026 to 7.0% from 1 Oct 2026, while paragraph (7) separately fixes medical cannabis at 6% — see the interaction VERIFY in Part 2.§47-2002; removal reporting (secondary): Outlaw Report

Law 24-332, Bill 24-113) added to §47-1803.03(a)(1): “provided, that a licensed medical cannabis business shall be allowed to, for the purposes of District income taxes, claim any tax deduction or credit that is prohibited for purposes of filing federal income taxes under §280E.” FindLaw shows that proviso as applicable only “upon the date of inclusion of the fiscal effect of amendments by D.C. Law 24-332 in an approved budget and financial plan,” while the DC Law Library current text read does not show the proviso — applicability/timing is VERIFY with OTR before relying on it (Part 2 §2.4).§47-1803.03 (FindLaw dual version); DC Law Library current text; enrolled Act B24-0113 (PDF)
0.11Hemp in DC is not a separate legal category. DC's controlled-substance definitions (§48-901.02(3)) contain no hemp exemption. OAG memorandum AL-21-222 (28 April 2021) concluded CBD, including hemp-derived CBD, falls within DC's cannabis definition. ABCA enforced that position against hemp shops in 2024–2026, including a 28 April 2026 Offer in Compromise (District Hemp Botanicals LLC, Case No. 25-ULC-00076) barring sale of “cannabis and cannabis products, including all cannabinoids, hemp, CBD, THCA, and THC” without a medical cannabis retail licence. No DC official response to the new federal hemp definition (P.L. 119-37 §781 / P.L. 119-103 §2019) was found — UNAVAILABLE.§48-901.02; District Hemp Offer in Compromise (PDF)

A July 2026 Council hearing covered four medical cannabis bills.LIMS B26-0654; LIMS B26-0522

Part 1 — Laws

1.1 Legal status: what is legal for adults, what is not, and why

Verbatim operative text — D.C. Code §48-904.01(a)(1) (current, DC Law Library):

“Except as authorized by this chapter or Chapter 16B of Title 7, it is unlawful for any person knowingly or intentionally to manufacture, distribute, or possess, with intent to manufacture or distribute, a controlled substance. Notwithstanding any provision of this chapter to the contrary, it shall be lawful, and shall not be an offense under District of Columbia law, for any person 21 years of age or older to: (A) Possess, use, purchase, or transport marijuana weighing 2 ounces or less; (B) Transfer to another person 21 years of age or older, without remuneration, marijuana weighing one ounce or less; (C) Possess, grow, harvest, or process, within the interior of a house or rental unit that constitutes such person's principal residence, no more than 6 cannabis plants, with 3 or fewer being mature, flowering plants; provided, that all persons residing within a single house or single rental unit may not possess, grow, harvest, or process, in the aggregate, more than 12 cannabis plants, with 6 or fewer being mature, flowering plants; (D) Possess within such house or rental unit the marijuana produced by such plants; provided that, nothing in this subsection shall make it lawful to sell, offer for sale, or make available for sale any marijuana or cannabis plants.”

— D.C. Code §48-904.01

Why sales are missing. Initiative 71 (2014) by its own terms legalised only possession, gifting and home grow; it never created a sales regime. The Harris rider (§1.3 below) then bars DC from spending any funds to enact a law that would create one. The two together produce the DC paradox: lawful to possess and to gift, unlawful to buy except as a medical patient. Note §48-904.01(a)(1A): marijuana ≤2 oz in an adult's personal possession is excluded from the Code's definition of “controlled substance” — but §48-904.01(a)(1A)(B) restores that definition for “any marijuana or cannabis plant sold or offered for sale or made available for sale.” Section (1B) bars a District agency from denying a facility, service, program or benefit because of conduct made lawful by the subsection; (1C) preserves employer/workplace policies; (1D) preserves DUI law; (1E) preserves private property owners' right to prohibit or regulate cannabis on their property.

Penalties that remain (same section). Simple possession in violation of §48-904.01(d) is a misdemeanour, up to 180 days and/or the fine in §22-3571.01; manufacturing, distributing or possessing with intent to distribute ≤½ lb of marijuana by a first-time offender is up to 180 days and/or fine; other Schedule I/II/III distribution offences carry up to 5 years (non-narcotic) — §48-904.01(a)(2), (d)(1). First-offence deferred-probation/expungement procedure is in §48-904.01(e).

Public consumption. The OAG FAQ (official) lists as “Not permitted”: smoking or otherwise consuming marijuana in a public space or in: A) a street, alley, park, sidewalk, or parking area; B) a vehicle in or upon any street, alley, park, or parking area; or C) any place to which the public is invited — plus possession/sale/transfer over 2 oz, any possession under 21, and selling or giving for money, goods or services. The enrolled 2014 act (B20-0409) made violation a misdemeanour punishable by a fine per §22-3571.01 or up to 60 days' imprisonment. The Code citation for the freestanding public-consumption offence is VERIFY — the OAG FAQ and enrolled act were relied on, not a current freestanding Code section. Sources: OAG FAQ; enrolled act B20-0409 (PDF).

Federal land caveat. Federal law still prohibits possession on federal land in DC (National Mall, Rock Creek Park, etc.) and federal officers may enforce there. This is a practical warning, not a DC Code provision — a general federal position, not DC-verified in this run.

1.2 Every DC statute and regulation on cannabis and hemp

A. The medical programme — D.C. Code Title 7, Chapter 16B (§§7-1671.01–7-1671.13), “Use of Marijuana for Medical Treatment”

Origin: Legalization of Marijuana for Medical Treatment Initiative of 1999 (Initiative 59, 1998 vote; implementing law D.C. Law 18-210, effective 27 July 2010; transmitted version D.C. Law 13-315, effective 25 February 2010 — both dates appear in different DC instruments; the chapter as codified cites the 1999 Initiative). Status: permanent chapter, as amended — but §§7-1671.01, 7-1671.06, 7-1671.06b and 7-1671.08 currently carry emergency amendments expiring 28 October 2026 (Act 26-391); the “Permanent Version” links on the Law Library show the text after those expire. Per-section last-amended dates are VERIFY on each section's own Law Library page. Full chapter: code.dccouncil.gov — Title 7, Chapter 16B.

CitationSubject — plain EnglishKey operative provisions
§7-1671.01Definitions“Cannabis” = §48-901.02(3); “medical cannabis” = cannabis cultivated/manufactured/possessed/distributed/dispensed/obtained/administered per Chapter 16B; “qualifying medical or dental condition” = “any condition for which treatment with medical cannabis would be beneficial, as determined by an authorized practitioner” (§7-1671.01(17)) — i.e. no closed list. “Social equity applicant” = applicant satisfying 2+ of: (A) DC-resident owner(s) ≥50% who are returning citizens; (B) DC-resident owner(s) ≥50% married/in civil union, parent/child/sibling/grandparent/non-parent guardian of a person arrested/convicted/incarcerated for a cannabis/drug offence; (C) DC-resident owner(s) ≥50% with income ≤150% of HUD median family income (household-size adjusted). “Returning citizen” = DC resident arrested, convicted or incarcerated for a cannabis/drug offence. “Affected ANC” = any ANC within 600 feet of the facility. “Unlicensed establishment” = business selling/exchanging as part of a commercial transaction/delivering cannabis/cannabis products (or, for §§7-1671.08(c-1),(g),(h),(i), Schedule I substances/products) from a specific DC location without an ABCA licence.
§7-1671.02Use of medical cannabisQualifying patient may purchase/possess/use/administer; caregiver may obtain/possess/dispense/administer/assist — only for a qualifying condition/treatment and only after (for 21+) self-certification or practitioner recommendation within the last 2 years + ABCA registration, or enrolment in another jurisdiction's programme (§7-1671.02(c)). Minors: practitioner recommendation + ABCA registration + parent/legal-guardian signed statement (§7-1671.02(e)). Caregiver registration needs no criminal background check (§7-1671.02(b-1)). Patients/caregivers may obtain only from an ABCA-licensed retailer/internet retailer (§7-1671.02(d)).
§7-1671.03Restrictions on use“(a)(1) The maximum amount of dried medical cannabis that any qualifying patient or caregiver may possess at any moment is 8 ounces.” The Mayor by rulemaking sets limits for non-dried forms. Administration only at: the patient's residence (if permitted); another's residence with permission (if permitted); a medical treatment facility (if permitted); an ABCA-licensed safe-use treatment facility; or the patient's school if it has a medication-administration policy and the product is non-smokable. Transport in a labelled container/sealed package per rulemaking. No operating/navigating/actual physical control of a motor vehicle, aircraft or motorboat under the influence.
§7-1671.04Recommending practitioner; protectionsAuthorised practitioner (physician, APRN, physician assistant, dentist, naturopathic physician — DC-licensed and in good standing) may recommend on an ABCA form after assessing current condition; no penalty/arrest/prosecution/discipline/denial of right or privilege for advising or recommending per the chapter; a practitioner may not have a professional office at a retailer/internet retailer/cultivation centre/manufacturer/testing laboratory, nor receive financial compensation from them.
§7-1671.05Medical cannabis programme (ABCA)Establishes the programme regulating “cultivation, manufacture, distribution, dispensing, purchase, delivery, sale, possession, administration, and testing of medical cannabis and the manufacture, distribution, purchase, sale, possession, and use of paraphernalia.” ABCA must: register patients (except other-jurisdiction enrollees), caregivers and non-resident cardholders; licence cultivation centres, manufacturers, retailers, internet retailers, couriers, testing laboratories (incl. directors/officers/members/incorporators/agents/employees); create the 21+ self-certification form (verbatim perjury statement in §1.5); permit temporary non-resident cards (3 days–1 year); issue non-transferable cards expiring every 2 years; require true, complete, real-time electronic records of employees, every transaction, on-hand quantity and disposal, with 24-hour written notice of loss/theft/destruction to ABCA and the MPD Chief; require batch segregation + testing-lab hold before sale (once ≥1 lab licensed); require labs to report THC/CBD concentration, organic/inorganic status, fertilisers/nutrients, mould/mildew/pests, mycotoxin/pesticides/heavy metals (and residual solvents for manufacturers) above Board thresholds; allow patient-sample testing; inspect (announced/unannounced) licensed and unlicensed establishments; run a sliding-scale dispensing system for patients who cannot afford supply; and submit an annual report to the Council covering patient/caregiver numbers, cards suspended/revoked, recommending practitioners, facility numbers/locations, harvest amounts, dollar sales by facility type, and violations (§7-1671.05(b)(20)).
§7-1671.05aProtests — pre-30 Jan 2024 unlicensed-establishment applicantsReferenced in §7-1671.06g(a) as the carve-out for protests against unlicensed establishments that applied before 30 January 2024. Full text not individually opened — VERIFY.
§7-1671.06Facilities, licences, caps, fees, locationSee the full licence table in Part 4. Core: (a) what each licence may do; (b) licence required before any activity; (e) conflicting-interest limits (no lab + any other licence; no retailer/internet/cultivation/lab/manufacturer + courier; max 2 cultivation licences; combined retailer+internet ≤3; manufacturers unlimited; cultivation holder max 1 retailer and no internet retailer); (h) ≥50% of new retailer, internet retailer, courier, cultivation and manufacturer licences issued after 22 Mar 2023 set aside for social equity applicants (with stated exceptions); (i) Board may by rulemaking cap or impose a moratorium by Ward/ANC/SMD after 22 Mar 2024, considering supply/demand; (k) conditional licence up to 3 years (Part 4); (m) social equity fee waiver up to 75% for the first 3 years (not endorsements); (n) annual fees, 3-year licence validity, $50/business-day late fee capped at licence cost, $100 returned-payment fee; (o) 8 oz/30 days dispensing cap; (p) no visibility from public/other property; (q)/(q-1)/(q-2) buffers (Part 3); (r) ownership-change approval; 3-year transfer bar for social-equity/unlicensed-establishment licences (except to a social equity applicant; otherwise repay grants/loans + waived fees); (s) DC-incorporated for-profit/nonprofit corporation, security plan (secure locked room, authorised persons only), employee compliance training; (u) no employee background check required with application — the Board may not inquire into or consider sealed/expunged/vacated/pardoned convictions, juvenile adjudications, non-conviction information, or convictions not directly related (with a 45-business-day response procedure and returning-citizen interest); (v) fine/suspend/revoke or warn; (w)–(y) 2021–22 open-period scoring (150+ points) conversions; (z) stipulated licence (ANC support/non-objection vote with quorum + fee; stops if a valid ANC protest is filed).
§7-1671.06aUnlicensed-establishment transition90-calendar-day open application period, no earlier than 180 days after 22 Mar 2023, for cultivation/retailer/internet retailer licences (eligibility in Part 4). ABCA must post the applicant list on its website + give a copy to DLCP; 45-day public comment; ANC protest on peace/order/quiet, parking/safety, property values; contested-case hearing within 120 days of a timely ANC protest; the 300-ft rule is inapplicable where a school/recreation-centre main entrance/nearest property line is on commercial/industrial-zoned ground.
§7-1671.06bUnlicensed-establishment compliancePending-application protection (except child-appealing products/packaging/ads cease-and-desist); (a-1) summary closure + padlock without prior hearing after 1 Apr 2025 if imminent danger per §7-1671.08(g)(2); Board approval in writing ≥15 days before issuance; unlicensed activity must cease on issuance; issuance deadlines (later of 30 Sep 2024 or 180 days from approval; retailer/internet retailer in operation by 31 Mar 2025 or approval rescinded/denied — historical deadlines, now passed); denial appeal (15 days to appeal, hearing + decision in 30 days; close within 30 days of final denial or face penalties); no display of cannabis prices/products on windows/doors/exterior.
§7-1671.06cSafe-use treatment facility endorsementRetailer only (not internet retailer). Designated consumption area separated by a secure door + separate ventilation; may sell for on-premises administration at time of purchase, allow removal of unused product (packaged per regs), sell non-cannabis food, offer recorded/background music. Must: keep 30-day security-camera recording, conspicuous warnings, destroy abandoned/unclaimed product, package/label per regs. Area isolated by walls + secure door, access only from the retailer; smoke-free employee monitoring area if combustibles offered; ventilation through pollution/odour control eliminating all detectable odour/smoke/combustion by-products. Must NOT: allow alcohol/tobacco; admit the public other than patient/caregiver; allow outside product (unless Board rulemaking permits); exceed quantity limits; run consumption games/contests awarding cannabis; advertise prohibited practices. Minimum initial fee $1,000; valid 3 years; minimum annual fee $2,000.
§7-1671.06dEducation tasting endorsementRetailer only. Cooking/how-to classes, demonstrations, tastings for educational purposes on-site; smoking only in a Board-approved safe-use facility. Must display warnings, destroy unconsumed product (except lawful take-home packaged per regs), label educational containers “not for sale”, no alcohol/tobacco, no prohibited-practice advertising, no unsubstantiated medical claims. Hours 7 a.m.–9 p.m., 7 days/week (Board may alter by rule). Board sets tasting/consumption limits by regulation. Minimum initial fee $130; 3 years; minimum annual fee $130.
§7-1671.06eRetailer delivery endorsement + internet retailer deliveriesRetailer delivery endorsement permits curbside pickup + delivery to residential/commercial DC addresses not on District/federal property or public/private school grounds (parks are neither). An internet retailer needs no delivery endorsement, but may NOT offer curbside pickup. Must: accept orders only from a patient/caregiver; deliver only to the patient/caregiver at the stated DC address, verifying identity, never leave product unverified; travel only through DC; follow Board rules on delivery frequency, overnight storage, vehicle numbers; obey landlord/property-owner delivery rules; use own employees or a licensed courier; verify government ID + ABCA registration at handover; vehicle with a secure locked compartment (cash and product in separate compartments); GPS active in the vehicle at all times; no markings/images/words indicating cannabis delivery. Delivery/curbside hours 9 a.m.–9 p.m., 7 days/week (Board may alter). Minimum initial fee $300; 3 years; minimum annual fee $300.
§7-1671.06fSummer garden endorsementRetailer + safe-use endorsement holders only; outdoor private-space sale/service/consumption. Hours 8 a.m.–midnight, 7 days/week. No summer garden endorsement if the retailer + safe-use facility is within 400 feet of a daycare centre. Minimum initial fee $300; 3 years; minimum annual fee $300.
§7-1671.06g–06jProtests / ANC weightStanding to protest retailer issuance/renewal/transfer: abutting property owner/commercial tenant; directly-across-the-street owner/tenant; daycare centre within 400 ft; any affected ANC. Written notice + specific grounds within the protest period. ABCA must give electronic notice to the ward Councilmember + any affected ANC ≥45 calendar days before approving a retailer location (§7-1671.06h); ANC + applicant may jointly request up to 30 extra calendar days to vote. Protest grounds (§7-1671.06i): violation of Chapter 16B; directly-related civil-law violation; vehicular/pedestrian safety. The Board may issue without a hearing if a protest lacks substance. The Board “shall accord great weight” to affected ANC input (§7-1671.06j).
§7-1671.08PenaltiesUnauthorised manufacture/cultivation/possession/administration/dispensing/distribution/use → criminal prosecution under Title 48, Ch. 11, Subch. I (§§48-1101 et seq.). Fraudulent representation to law enforcement to avoid arrest/prosecution: criminal fine ≤$1,000 (plus other penalties). (c-1) violation for ANY licensed or unlicensed establishment, even with a pending application, to sell/exchange as part of a commercial transaction/deliver Schedule I substances or products containing them. The Board may impose/adjudicate civil fines on licensed/unlicensed facilities. Unlicensed-establishment fines from 31 Jan 2024: 1st $10,000 (+ remediation plan); 2nd $20,000; 3rd+ $30,000; failure to submit / rejected remediation plan $10,000; fine revenue to the General Fund. Summary closure/padlock/seizure (g): if inspection finds imminent danger — defined to include sale/attempted sale to under-21s; on-premises manufacture/production/cultivation; (once a lab is licensed) distribution of untested product; unlabelled/mislabelled product (no source, contents, ingredients, THC content); failure to comply with a cease-and-desist; distribution of Schedule I substances per §48-902.04; unlawful firearms/weapons on premises; dangerous crime / crime of violence on premises; any Board-by-rule imminent danger. Owner + property owner get written notice + hearing right; owner has 5 business days to request a hearing; the Board hears within 5 business days and decides within 5 business days (10 if no hearing requested); closure may continue until the danger is abated and, if the Board reasonably judges recurrence likely, thereafter. (g-1) extends the same summary power to licensed establishments for (g)(2)(C)–(G) reasons (added by Act 26-391). ABCA investigators may test cannabis/products for THC content and, on probable cause, other substances for Schedule I presence; unlicensed-establishment labelling stating cannabis/THC content creates a presumption of that content.
§7-1671.08aMedical Cannabis Administration FundAll medical cannabis licensing/permitting/registration fees deposited; used by ABCA to administer the programme; year-end remainder transferred to the General Fund unassigned balance; penalties/fines under §7-1671.08 credited to the General Fund unassigned balance.
§7-1671.08b / 08cSocial Equity Fund; equity grants/loansBoth shown as [Repealed] in the current chapter text. Do NOT cite the pre-repeal redirection of sales-tax proceeds to the Social Equity Fund as current law (see Part 2). Repeal date/instrument VERIFY.
§7-1671.09Advisory CommitteeThe Mayor shall establish a Medical Cannabis Advisory Committee monitoring other states' best practices, scientific research, and programme effectiveness.
§§7-1671.10–12Fees; liability; insuranceABCA may by rulemaking set facility licensing + inspection/audit fees; fees applied first to administration cost. No liability on duly authorised District enforcement officers by virtue of the chapter; the District is not liable for deleterious outcomes of medical cannabis use. Nothing requires any health insurer/plan to reimburse medical cannabis.
§§7-1671.12a–12eEnforcement machineryCease-and-desist (pre-hearing, immediate/irreparable harm; hearing request 15 days, decision 90 days; expedited hearing 10 days, decision 30 days; final if no timely request). Premises/books/records examination any time during business hours; ABCA investigators examine each licensed establishment at least once per year (with reasonable advance notice efforts). Search warrants for illegal cannabis products + disposition by court. DLCP, OTR and FEMS must notify the Board within 30 days of any citation/revocation/enforcement action against a licensed establishment; MPD must file incident reports within 30 days (public on request). Nuisance: any unlicensed establishment selling/exchanging/delivering/permitting consumption is a nuisance (except accepted pending 90-day-period applicants); the AG may sue in Superior Court Civil Branch; preliminary-injunction hearing within 14 days; the District need not prove irreparable harm.
§7-1671.13RulemakingBoard rulemaking authority (including the Council-review mechanism for testing rules — see the December 2025 remediation final rule in §1.2E, deemed Council-approved 19 Nov 2025, Board-adopted 3 Dec 2025). Full text not individually quoted — VERIFY for the 30-day deemed-approval detail beyond that rulemaking notice.

B. Controlled substances — Title 48

  • §48-901.02(3) — “Cannabis” = “all parts of the plant genus Cannabis, including both marijuana and hashish,” with marijuana defined to exclude resin/hashish, mature stalks, stalk fibre, seed oil/cake and derivatives thereof, and sterilised seed incapable of germination. There is no hemp exclusion in this definition. §48-901.02
  • §48-902.04 — Schedule I list (referenced by §7-1671.01(20B-i) and §7-1671.08(g)(2)(F)). Text not individually opened — VERIFY for which cannabinoids are expressly listed (secondary sources say delta-8 THC is named; that was not confirmed on the official section text in this run).
  • §48-904.01 — Initiative 71 possession/gifting/grow (§1.1 above); penalties at §48-904.01(a)(2), (d).
  • Title 48, Chapter 11, Subchapter I (§§48-1101 et seq.) — criminal sanctions for unauthorised medical-cannabis activity, via §7-1671.08(a). Not individually opened — VERIFY.

C. Tax — Title 47

§47-2002(a)(7) (6% medical cannabis sales tax; FY2026 deposit rule) and §47-1803.03(a)(1) (§280E decoupling proviso, conditional version) — see Part 2. §47-1801.04(31A) defines “licensed medical cannabis business” (cultivation centre, manufacturer, retailer, internet retailer, courier, testing laboratory licensed with ABCA per §7-1671.06(b)) — per enrolled B24-0113.

D. Employment — D.C. Law 24-190

Cannabis Employment Protections Amendment Act of 2022 (signed by the Mayor 13 July 2022; effective 13 September 2022 per secondary summary — effective/applicability date VERIFY on the Law Library page). §102(a): “An employer may not refuse to hire, terminate from employment, suspend, fail to promote, demote, or penalize an individual based upon: (1) The individual's use of cannabis; (2) The individual's status as a medical cannabis program patient; or (3) The presence of cannabinoid metabolites in the individual's bodily fluids in an employer-required or requested drug test without additional factors indicating impairment pursuant to subsection (b)(4).” Exceptions (§102(b)): safety-sensitive positions (as designated by the employer); action required by federal statute/regulation/contract/funding agreement; use/possession at the workplace, while performing work, or during work hours (unless permitted under HRA §211(b-1) / CMPA §2062 for medical patients); impairment (specific articulable symptoms substantially decreasing performance or interfering with a safe/healthy workplace duty). Codified section number VERIFY — do not cite a §32-xxxx number from memory. Law page: code.dccouncil.gov — Law 24-190. See also §1.9.

Part 1700; no statement/depiction/illustration that promotes overconsumption, depicts a person under 21 consuming cannabis, or is “especially appealing to persons under 21” (cartoon characters/toys/animals; bubble/cartoon-like font; design/brand/name resembling a non-cannabis consumer product; symbols/celebrities commonly used to market to under-21s; the word “candy” or “candies”). §7-1671.06(c-1) — violation for any licensed/unlicensed facility to represent goods/services/business as compliant with Initiative 71 (except licensed facilities may have signage indicating medical cannabis is on the property).

Remediation-testing final rulemaking (PDF)
